2026-09-02 | tennis | G38 | jump_p95 DIAGNOSED as a player-SELECTION defect, not a metric artifact. Probe reproduces the harness exactly on 2 of 4 clips (tennis_03 34.36 and tennis_04 10.03 to the digit; tennis_05 36.75 vs 36.79 and tennis_02 21.67 vs 22.13 differ only by quantile interpolation). TWO HYPOTHESES RAISED AND FALSIFIED: (a) ball contamination -- track 99 is the ball and its projected x reaches 106,853 ft with single transitions of 126,001 and 760,419 ft, but tracking_harness.py:161 computes coverage/oob/jump on cls==player only, so the gates are clean and the harness is correct; (b) re-appearance after a frame gap -- real but secondary, since on 3 of 4 clips the MAJORITY of >8 ft jumps are stride-adjacent at gap=3 (65.4 / 64.3 / 56.7 pct; tennis_02 is the exception at 30.4 pct). THE ACTUAL CAUSE: the >8 ft mass sits in the 10-29 ft band (188+102 of 508 on tennis_03), not at court length -- full end-swaps are only 5.9-7.6 pct although jump_max is 77-86 ft on every clip, which is one court length. At stride 3 and 30 fps a 10-29 ft displacement is 100-290 ft/s against about 39 ft/s for a world-class sprint, so it is one to two orders of magnitude beyond human motion and cannot be movement, blur or smoothing. Structural enabler: every clip has EXACTLY two player tracks with EXACTLY equal row counts (1490/1490, 954/954, 295/295, 2273/2273), so the adapter emits one player per half per sampled frame and the instability is a per-half CHOICE, not a multi-track association. Relationship to G26 is CONSISTENT BUT NOT PROVEN: only 4.9-13.0 pct of big jumps have an endpoint outside the generous G26 rectangle, which is weak counter-evidence that does not settle it because ball kids and line judges stand inside that box. G26 attempt 2 is the experiment that settles it; do not fix G38 before it reports. No renders viewed -- numerical diagnosis by elimination, and any fix pass must carry render-and-look | MEASURED (diagnosis; no fix attempted) | census + probe, no code change

IMPLICATION: no whole-clip decoded-frame coverage above ~0.42 is reachable on this broadcast, so the frozen 0.90 coverage bar is unreachable on decoded frames by more than 2x, as a property of broadcast tennis rather than a solver defect. SECOND AND LARGER FINDING: the harness does NOT use a decoded-frame denominator. tracking_harness.py:146 sets n_frames = df["frame"].nunique() and lines 163-169 divide coverage, det_per_frame and ball_valid by it, so frames where the tracker emitted NOTHING are excluded from the denominator by construction; the string "decoded" appears nowhere in the file, while the program rail repeated in the register header, the research plan, docs/TRACKING.md and every session prompt says denominators are decoded frames. Measured on the four tennis tables whose row counts match the daemon census exactly: coverage as the harness reports it 0.1512 / 0.4393 / 0.5999 / 0.3284 (reproducing the census 0.15 / 0.44 / 0.60 / 0.33 exactly) versus coverage on processable frames at the observed stride 3 of 0.0309 / 0.1585 / 0.2372 / 0.1200 -- an inflation of 4.90x / 2.77x / 2.53x / 2.74x. It has never manufactured a PASS (every clip already fails and the correction makes numbers worse), but it is the B1 pattern: the hardest frames, the ones with no detections at all, are dropped from the denominator rather than counted as uncovered. Tested whether the oob rows and the big-jump transitions are the same rows, using the harness tennis bounds (-21, 99, -12, 48) from tracking_harness.py:37. Result on the four clips whose row counts match the daemon census: oob_rows = 0 of 590 / 2,980 / 4,546 / 1,908 player rows -- ZERO on every clip -- while those same clips carry 46 / 508 / 254 / 370 big jumps over 8 ft. 2026-09-02 | all | G41 | LIVE INCIDENT FOUND AND FIXED: the pod rebuild silently broke every Hough-based line detector in FOUR sports. OpenCV 5 returns cv2.HoughLinesP as shape (N, 4) where OpenCV 4 returns (N, 1, 4), and every call site in this repo indexes [:, 0, :], so all of them raised IndexError under the cv2 5.0.0 that the post-reallocation env rebuild installed. Confirmed sites: domains/tennis/tracking/court_lines.py:96, domains/soccer/tracking/geometry.py:101, domains/soccer/tracking/keypoints.py:50, domains/football/tracking/geometry.py:113, domains/football/tracking/line_probe.py:45, domains/football/tracking/clustering_diagnostic.py:36, domains/basketball/tracking/line_calibration.py:62, scripts/platformkit/football_content_gate.py:53. WHY IT WAS DANGEROUS: it did not look like a crash. The daemon kept running and kept writing tables that were nearly empty -- a tennis re-track produced 5 rows instead of thousands -- and test_track_daemon.py passed 28/28 throughout because it never exercises a Hough path, so a green per-file suite was NOT evidence the environment was sound. It was caught only because a sequential-plan run raised the IndexError in a log I read. RESTORED: pod pinned to opencv-python-headless<5, now cv2 4.14.0, HoughLinesP shape verified 3-dimensional, and tennis detection verified end to end on tennis_09 frame 6960 -- 106 segments, detect_court gate accepted. The durable code fix (reshape(-1, shape[-1]), correct for BOTH layouts, plus a test feeding both shapes) is dispatched as gap G41. Lesson saved to memory as pod_rebuild_breaks_cv2_hough_2026_09_02 | LANDED (ops fix; code hardening dispatched) | pod pinned cv2 4.14.0

2026-09-02 | tennis | G39 | BALL PROJECTION BLOW-UP: CAUSE ESTABLISHED, leading hypothesis FALSIFIED. Premise reproduced exactly (tennis_03 ball x max 106,853.7 ft with a 126,001.0 ft step; tennis_05 step 760,419.9 ft) and it is not a tail -- only 24.5 / 64.6 / 53.4 / 35.3 pct of ball rows land inside a generous court rectangle. THE HOMOGRAPHY IS NOT NEAR-SINGULAR: cond(H) flat to four significant figures between well-behaved and blown-up rows (nyYk 44,665 vs 44,707; 3x3 21,550 vs 21,550), and every emitted ball row carries fresh or drift-checked provenance, so stale calibration is out too. Projection law confirmed against real image coordinates: 1/|court_x| tracks the ball pixel distance to the ground-plane vanishing line at r = 0.9654 (n=188) and r = 0.8853 (n=191). THE CAUSE: the detection is not the ball -- 12 of 12 evenly spaced renders over the offending set show 9 far-player heads/bodies/rackets and 3 crowd-or-scoreboard blobs, zero tennis balls, with the real yellow ball plainly visible elsewhere in 4 of them. MotionDiffDetector's only spatial rule is y < 2/3*height, which excludes the near half of the court and admits backdrop, crowd and scoreboard; ball_rows then projects that off-plane pixel through the ground-plane homography with no plane check and no bound. Magnitude is set by where the horizon sits: on the two re-runnable clips the vanishing row is off-screen (-240, -109) so the worst values are only 185 and 201 ft, while tennis_02-05 carry 34-70 rows per clip below -1,000 ft, a sign flip only producible by a pixel beyond the horizon. CORRECTION TO G38: its claim that players in the SAME frames stay within 82.93 ft is UNSUPPORTED -- blow-up frames carry zero player rows on three of four clips (1 of 727 rows overall) | MEASURED (cause established; hypothesis falsified; G38 claim corrected) | g39_ball_projection_diagnosis_2026-09-02.md

2026-09-02 | tennis | G42 | CAUSE ESTABLISHED, AND IT REVERSES THE FRAMING: the tennis 'collapse' was the FIX WORKING, not a regression. Decisive artifact found during premise reproduction -- every HEALTHY table has a 5-column header (frame,track_id,cls,x,y) and every DEGENERATE table has a 9-column header adding calibration_provenance,coordinate_space,observation,calibration, which come from write_csv in domains/tennis/tracking/adapter.py, so the daemon provably was not running the same adapter at 06:04 and at 17:14. Cause: commit f16b3863a (2026-09-01 15:32 UTC, squarely inside the 07:39-17:14 gap) made the adapter return None instead of the PREVIOUS frame's homography when the current solve fails. Before it, an unsolved frame reused a stale homography and emitted rows stamped as fresh; after it, an unsolved frame emits nothing. The repo's own measurement of that inflation is docs/TRACKING.md:34 -- coverage 0.976 was 99.3 pct carried-over calibration against an honest 0.0067, a 145.7x inflation -- and SESSION_2026-09-01_INDEX.md:21 records 0 of 725 fresh-solve accepts inside the degenerate window itself. RULED OUT WITH EVIDENCE: daemon settings (the log shows 24 active in BOTH windows; no CV_*/MAX_*/STRIDE/TIMEOUT/MODEL in the daemon environ; cwd correct), weights (yolov8n.pt present, no CV_DETECTOR_MODEL, detection demonstrably works on the same clips today), and cv2 (16 h too late, reconfirmed independently). TWO SECONDARY CONTRIBUTORS, measured not assumed: re-tracking tennis_3x3eEWCZmWQ under the identical budget as the tennis_09 control gave 70 rows against 313, and the two highest-scoring degenerate clips are both 640x360, so resolution is worth about 4.5x; and every degenerate clip spans 300 s / ~7,500 frames against healthy tables spanning to frame 28,770, about 4x shorter. Neither reaches three orders of magnitude and neither explains a header-only file. SEPARATE REAL FINDING: a 2700 s job timeout landed at 09:49 UTC and the ledger shows tennis_06-09 each killed twice at ~2700 s with rows=0, while every healthy run took 4,827-8,773 s -- so NO healthy tennis run would survive the current 2700 s or later 3600 s budget | MEASURED (cause established; framing reversed) | g42_tennis_collapse_cause_2026-09-02.md

2026-09-02 | soccer | G17 | ROLE GROUND TRUTH BUILT (300 crops), AND IT INVALIDATES THE ACCEPTANCE BAR BEFORE ANY MODEL TRAINS ON IT. Labels: player 268 (89.3 pct), other 25 (8.3 pct), referee 7 (2.3 pct), in docs/evidence/tracking/soccer_roles_labels/ with crops, contact sheets, labels.csv, a per-frame count table and a README. Sampling was seeded (20260902) and deliberately unbiased: 130 source frames (100 sealed S1 packet frames plus 30 G08 stream-window renders), seeded-shuffled frame order, round-robin one box per frame per pass with the in-frame box choice also seeded-random rather than confidence-sorted, giving 78 frames at 2 crops, 46 at 3, 6 at 1 and a per-clip spread of 96-102 -- no head slice and no confidence bias. Boxes were generated fresh on the pod through build_soccer_packet_detector (pinned yolov8n, deterministic path) because the sealed CSVs hold counts only, no boxes; no sealed file was modified. 36 of 300 crops (12 pct) are documented individually as ambiguous with the call and the reasoning, including 9 detector false positives (ball, ad board, card, graphic) folded into `other` because only three classes were specified. THE ADJUDICATION: G17 v3's pre-stated bar is held-out crop accuracy >= 0.90, but the MAJORITY-CLASS BASELINE IS 0.8933 -- a classifier that always answers `player` and never identifies a single referee scores 0.8933, just 0.0067 under the bar. The bar therefore cannot separate a useful model from a trivial one, which is contract B1/B9 territory (a metric a degenerate strategy satisfies). Compounding it, 7 referee examples over 5-fold CV is 1.4 per fold, so the referee class is not learnable at this sample size regardless of architecture. The bar is NOT being lowered and NOT being raised; per-class recall is being ADDED as a required companion metric, and the referee half is flagged as likely CLOSED AT LIMIT on label scarcity rather than on model capability | MEASURED (ground truth built; acceptance rule corrected before use) | soccer_roles_labels/

TWO INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATIONS, neither using the length axis: (a) painted WIDTH, pinned by the near corners and immune to a length mislabel -- the line the solver calls far measures 36.2 ft wide spanning -0.2..36.1 ft, a doubles baseline, in 91 of 91 frames, while the far service line is separately detected at 60.0 solver-ft and 26.8 ft wide spanning 4.5..31.4 ft (singles width 27 ft) in 89 of 91; (b) the painted centre service line, which physically ends at the far service line at a true 60 ft, reads 59.49 solver-ft median (sd 0.24) where the premise would require 78 -- and detection gaps can only pull that DOWN, so the test was conservative against confirming. Render tally 20 of 20 viewed: 20/20 put the 78 ft edge on the painted far baseline and 0/20 on the far service line, with 20/20 also placing the 60 ft line on the far service line and the 39 ft line at the net base. Coverage 4 clips, 3 cameras, 2 surfaces, evenly spaced across each whole clip, deliberately including two frames inside G39's own 5501-5902 window. WHY G39 READ IT THAT WAY: the far service line (on the ground at 60 ft) and the net's top tape sit within about 10 image rows in this camera family, and the tape is 3 ft off-plane so it projects to about 54 solver-ft -- an overlay showing 78 plus that pair reads as a box stopping at a service line. CONSEQUENCE: the 5.28 ft classical anchor and the G23 pseudo-labels carry NO 1.3x scale error, and G31 was not trained on length-biased labels. Chained with the G34 rally share of 0.417 that is about 14 pct of decoded frames today, against roughly 27 pct if the gate were widened. The orchestrator called it an anomaly that 'both interventions ADDED candidates and recall FELL, which should not happen'. CLAHE regenerates the whole LSD proposal set rather than adding to it (witness wnba_05 frame 6912 lane_right: segments 77 -> 82, groups 58 -> 57, and the group that previously matched the true line no longer matches), and G120's merge replaces fragment spans, changing group geometry. The net +4.4 pct candidate volume concealed a CHANGED underlying set. Once that is seen, a recall drop is not paradoxical. Mechanism distribution over 10 loss records covering 9 physical lines: CLAHE changing the upstream LSD proposal set 7 (70 pct), G120 replacement/fragment-group geometry 3 (30 pct), and greedy correspondence claiming, top-N eviction and baseline non-determinism 0 (0 pct) -- all three explicitly ruled out. IMPORTANTLY, the baseline reproduced at 25/68 TWICE, so the pipeline is deterministic and the G120 and G123 REJECTs both stand rather than being noise, which was the outcome that would have invalidated them. IMPLICATION for the next attempt: a purely ADDITIVE approach -- unioning candidates from the original and enhanced images rather than substituting one for the other -- has not been tried and is the design the mechanism actually points at. That is G132. Evidence: g129_why_more_candidates_loses_recall memo and lost_line_traces.csv with 10 side-by-side renders.

Unioning the original and CLAHE-enhanced LSD segment sets RAISED recall for the first time, 25/68 = 36.76 pct to 28/68 = 41.18 pct, at a paired-precision cost of 13.41 -> 11.17 pct. But the spec required verifying the additive property directly, and it FAILED: only **24 of the 25 baseline matches survived**. A union of segments that still loses a previously matched line is not additive, which refutes the design justification the orchestrator wrote the row on. WHAT THAT ISOLATES: the non-additive stage is not segment proposal, it is GROUPING. Three interventions have now perturbed the proposal set (G120 merge, G123 CLAHE replacement, G132 union) and all three were disturbed by the same downstream step -- candidate_line_group_details re-partitions over whatever segments it is given, so a group that matched a true line can stop matching even when every original segment is still present. G129 had already traced 70 pct of losses to proposal-set change and 30 pct to group geometry; G132 shows grouping alone suffices. THE NEXT ATTACK IS THE GROUPING STAGE, not the detector -- that is G134. Also worth recording honestly: this is the first recall GAIN measured, and it is being rejected on a design-integrity ground rather than on the headline number, which is the correct call but should be revisited if grouping is made stable. 2026-09-02 | basketball | G135 | End-to-end solve attempted: **0 qualifying all-four-line frames out of 30**. No homography, external feet validation or reprojection render was fabricated; the lane reported the empty result, which the spec named in advance as legitimate. HOW TO READ THE ZERO -- it is not a refutation: at G134's recall of 30/68 = 44.12 pct the implied all-four co-occurrence is 3.79 pct, so 30 frames predict 1.14 qualifying frames and P(observe 0) = 0.321. A one-in-three outcome is not evidence against the method; the 30-frame frozen sample is too small to test solvability. Sample sizes that WOULD test it: 131 frames to expect >= 5 qualifiers, 200 frames for expected 7.58 at P(0) = 0.001. CAVEAT THAT CUTS AGAINST CONVENIENCE: the four lines are almost certainly POSITIVELY correlated, since a frame showing the paint well shows all four and one that does not shows none, so true co-occurrence likely exceeds the independent 3.79 pct -- which makes observing zero slightly MORE surprising than the model says, though still not decisive at n=30. CAUSE, reproduced rather than inferred: MPEG-TS exposes a selected stream both inside its program and in the top-level stream list, so ffprobe emits the identical frame count TWICE. FIX: deduplicate only IDENTICAL reports; differing values still reject, because differing counts are genuinely ambiguous and must not select a stream by position. 2026-09-02 | all | OPS | Bridge throughput restored a SECOND time, and this one was the real stall. After the yt-dlp upgrade fixed downloads, the bridge still moved nothing for an hour: 23,074 MB of local staging grew 0 KB in 100 s while 80 yt-dlp/ffmpeg processes ran. CAUSE, traced through the attempt ladder rather than guessed: for any video over about 20 minutes the ladder plans a 16-minute SECTION and tries all EIGHT section attempts before any full-file attempt. Both routes to a section are dead. WITH cookies the ladder uses the default player client, whose media URL returns 403 to anything but yt-dlp itself, and ffmpeg does the section cut -- the exact failure footage_bridge.py:208-211 already documented. WITHOUT cookies it forces player_client=web, which now yields ZERO media formats: measured live as web 0, default 31. So every section attempt was a guaranteed extraction plus ffmpeg 403. FIX: SECTIONS_ENABLED = False, with the measurement recorded in the code so re-enabling requires re-measuring. VERIFIED: staging grew **51 MB in 6 s** immediately after, against 0 KB in 100 s before. Full downloads were never broken -- the same video fetched 540 MB on rung 2 -- and full games are what the pipeline wants anyway. The section code and its five tests are retained, now enabling the flag explicitly; 43 tests pass. SEPARATE ISSUE FOUND AND CLEARED: 40 yt-dlp plus 40 ffmpeg orphans had accumulated from repeated worker kills (footage_bridge.py:85 records this box crashing twice from such a pileup); bridge_keeper now reports any count above 24 and defaults to --per-lane 1.

But I also specified a UNIFORM sample of 180 frames over 174,430, which is a stride of 969.06, so a 3,801-frame span can only ever receive **3,801 / 969.06 = 3.92 samples.** The row got **exactly four** (19865 a, 20834 a, 21803 a, 22772 c) and **correctly refused to call it.** **That arithmetic was available to me before dispatch and I did not do it.** The right design was to oversample the studied span as a separate stratum, and **the question remains OPEN.** **FLAW 2 -- THE 40/40 AGREEMENT CHECKS THE EASY BOUNDARY, NOT THE ONE THAT DETERMINES THE ANSWER.** I required a re-judge of 40 frames but did not require it to be STRATIFIED across categories.
